Um... this has been going on for about a month now. It started right after my sister died. It was completely unexpected, and I guess it's just been really hard to process.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure I would use the word depressed, really. I mean, I miss her, obviously, but I haven't noticed feeling down most days. It's really just... those mornings I mentioned where I have those thoughts. Once I actually get out of bed and get going, I don't feel sad all day.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But lately, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. It's hard to explain. And even my hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... they just don't give me that little lift they used to.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, have you ever had another period in your life when you lost interest in things the way you have this past month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. Um... I've always been someone who stays pretty busy and involved in things. I mean, everyone has an off day here and there, but... no, I can't remember ever losing interest like this before she passed. This is really the first time.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, has it been harder for you to manage your usual day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been. Um... for example, I help put together a Juneteenth budgeting workshop, and recently I've had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of it for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And... well, I've also been turning down my neighbors and friends a lot more than usual lately when they ask for help or want to get together. It's just taking a bit too much out of me right now.\n\nDoctor: When you do feel sad, is it usually when something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's definitely worse when I think of her, but... the sadness is just kind of there all the time. It doesn't really matter what I'm doing or if I'm thinking about her or not. It's just this constant heaviness.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Food just... it doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll usually just fix a little toast or maybe some soup, and then I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything all day. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything like that, I just don't have much of a desire to eat right now.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, I kind of just fall asleep in bits and pieces. And then I end up waking up way before dawn, with my mind already turning. Once I'm awake like that, I usually just can't get settled again before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, maybe not worthless exactly, but... I do feel a lot of guilt. I just keep going over those last few weeks with my sister. I keep telling myself that I should have called her more, or listened better... just done something more useful for her. Even though part of me logically knows I couldn't have changed what happened, I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done better by her.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel responsible for your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I know I didn't cause it... her passing wasn't my fault. It's just, well, like I was saying, it's more about wishing I had been a better sister to her at the end. But no, I don't actually feel responsible for the fact that she died.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have moments where I miss her terribly, of course, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day, every day. It comes and goes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It's just this constant heaviness.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Food just... it doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll usually just fix a little toast or maybe some soup, and then I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything all day. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything like that, I just don't have much of a desire to eat right now.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, I kind of just fall asleep in bits and pieces. And then I end up waking up way before dawn, with my mind already turning. Once I'm awake like that, I usually just can't get settled again before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, maybe not worthless exactly, but... I do feel a lot of guilt. I just keep going over those last few weeks with my sister. I keep telling myself that I should have called her more, or listened better... just done something more useful for her. Even though part of me logically knows I couldn't have changed what happened, I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done better by her.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel responsible for your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I know I didn't cause it... her passing wasn't my fault. It's just, well, like I was saying, it's more about wishing I had been a better sister to her at the end. But no, I don't actually feel responsible for the fact that she died.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have moments where I miss her terribly, of course, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day, every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling sad most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned just a moment ago, no, not really. I don't feel sad all day, every day. It just comes in waves when I miss her, but it isn't a constant thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But just having that thought cross my mind, and having it keep happening... I figured it was time I came in to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Did these thoughts start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it did. Um... this has been going on for about a month now. It started right after my sister died. It was completely unexpected, and I guess it's just been really hard to process.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure I would use the word depressed, really. I mean, I miss her, obviously, but I haven't noticed feeling down most days. It's really just... those mornings I mentioned where I have those thoughts. Once I actually get out of bed and get going, I don't feel sad all day.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But lately, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. It's hard to explain. And even my hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... they just don't give me that little lift they used to.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, have you ever had another period in your life when you lost interest in things the way you have this past month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. Um... I've always been someone who stays pretty busy and involved in things. I mean, everyone has an off day here and there, but... no, I can't remember ever losing interest like this before she passed. This is really the first time.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, has it been harder for you to manage your usual day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been. Um... for example, I help put together a Juneteenth budgeting workshop, and recently I've had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of it for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And... well, I've also been turning down my neighbors and friends a lot more than usual lately when they ask for help or want to get together. It's just taking a bit too much out of me right now.\n\nDoctor: When you do feel sad, is it usually when something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's definitely worse when I think of her, but... the sadness is just kind of there all the time. It doesn't really matter what I'm doing or if I'm thinking about her or not. It's just this constant heaviness.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Food just... it doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll usually just fix a little toast or maybe some soup, and then I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything all day. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything like that, I just don't have much of a desire to eat right now.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, I kind of just fall asleep in bits and pieces. And then I end up waking up way before dawn, with my mind already turning. Once I'm awake like that, I usually just can't get settled again before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, maybe not worthless exactly, but... I do feel a lot of guilt. I just keep going over those last few weeks with my sister. I keep telling myself that I should have called her more, or listened better... just done something more useful for her. Even though part of me logically knows I couldn't have changed what happened, I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done better by her.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel responsible for your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I know I didn't cause it... her passing wasn't my fault. It's just, well, like I was saying, it's more about wishing I had been a better sister to her at the end. But no, I don't actually feel responsible for the fact that she died.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have moments where I miss her terribly, of course, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day, every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling sad most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned just a moment ago, no, not really. I don't feel sad all day, every day. It just comes in waves when I miss her, but it isn't a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your sister’s death has been more intense than you would have expected for yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I think... well, losing a sister is just a hard thing to go through. My reaction feels pretty normal to me. I expected to grieve and to miss her, and that's exactly what's happening. So, no, it really hasn't been more intense than I would have thought.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, I move around at my normal pace... well, normal for me, anyway. And my speaking hasn't changed at all. I'm pretty much exactly the same in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you found yourself longing or yearning for her a lot?\n\nPatient: I mean, I miss her, certainly. But... I wouldn't really use the words longing or yearning, not constantly anyway. It's just the normal amount of missing someone after they pass away. I'm not consumed by it or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Um... for example, I help put together a Juneteenth budgeting workshop, and recently I've had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of it for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And... well, I've also been turning down my neighbors and friends a lot more than usual lately when they ask for help or want to get together. I'm pretty much exactly the same in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you found yourself longing or yearning for her a lot?\n\nPatient: I mean, I miss her, certainly. But... I mean, it's definitely worse when I think of her, but... the sadness is just kind of there all the time. It doesn't really matter what I'm doing or if I'm thinking about her or not. It's just this constant heaviness.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Food just... it doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll usually just fix a little toast or maybe some soup, and then I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything all day. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything like that, I just don't have much of a desire to eat right now.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, I kind of just fall asleep in bits and pieces. And then I end up waking up way before dawn, with my mind already turning. Once I'm awake like that, I usually just can't get settled again before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, maybe not worthless exactly, but... I do feel a lot of guilt. I just keep going over those last few weeks with my sister. I keep telling myself that I should have called her more, or listened better... just done something more useful for her. Even though part of me logically knows I couldn't have changed what happened, I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done better by her.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel responsible for your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I know I didn't cause it... her passing wasn't my fault. It's just, well, like I was saying, it's more about wishing I had been a better sister to her at the end. But no, I don't actually feel responsible for the fact that she died.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have moments where I miss her terribly, of course, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day, every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling sad most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned just a moment ago, no, not really. I don't feel sad all day, every day. It just comes in waves when I miss her, but it isn't a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your sister’s death has been more intense than you would have expected for yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I think... well, losing a sister is just a hard thing to go through. My reaction feels pretty normal to me. I expected to grieve and to miss her, and that's exactly what's happening. So, no, it really hasn't been more intense than I would have thought.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, I move around at my normal pace... well, normal for me, anyway. And my speaking hasn't changed at all. I'm pretty much exactly the same in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you found yourself longing or yearning for her a lot?\n\nPatient: I mean, I miss her, certainly. But... I wouldn't really use the words longing or yearning, not constantly anyway. It's just the normal amount of missing someone after they pass away. I'm not consumed by it or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I have noticed that. I'll sit down to look at my budgeting spreadsheet, or try to read something for a workshop I'm taking, and... I'll just stare at it. I can reread the same paragraph three or four times and still not really take in what it says. It's just hard to focus.\n\nDoctor: When something good happens, are you still able to feel better, even for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night, but... lately I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Even the things I used to really enjoy, like working in my garden or cooking for the neighbors, they just don't give me that little lift they used to. I just feel kind of flat, no matter what's going on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know I didn't cause it... her passing wasn't my fault. It's just, well, like I was saying, it's more about wishing I had been a better sister to her at the end. But no, I don't actually feel responsible for the fact that she died.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have moments where I miss her terribly, of course, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day, every day. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling sad most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned just a moment ago, no, not really. I don't feel sad all day, every day. It just comes in waves when I miss her, but it isn't a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your sister’s death has been more intense than you would have expected for yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I think... well, losing a sister is just a hard thing to go through. My reaction feels pretty normal to me. I expected to grieve and to miss her, and that's exactly what's happening. So, no, it really hasn't been more intense than I would have thought.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, I move around at my normal pace... well, normal for me, anyway. And my speaking hasn't changed at all. I'm pretty much exactly the same in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you found yourself longing or yearning for her a lot?\n\nPatient: I mean, I miss her, certainly. But... I wouldn't really use the words longing or yearning, not constantly anyway. It's just the normal amount of missing someone after they pass away. I'm not consumed by it or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I have noticed that. I'll sit down to look at my budgeting spreadsheet, or try to read something for a workshop I'm taking, and... I'll just stare at it. I can reread the same paragraph three or four times and still not really take in what it says. It's just hard to focus.\n\nDoctor: When something good happens, are you still able to feel better, even for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night, but... lately I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Even the things I used to really enjoy, like working in my garden or cooking for the neighbors, they just don't give me that little lift they used to. I just feel kind of flat, no matter what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Since your sister died, have you been feeling unusually tired or low in energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really have. It's... well, even doing simple errands takes it out of me lately. Or just trying to get my papers together for the Cultural Center, which shouldn't be hard at all, but it just leaves me feeling completely worn out. It honestly feels like my body is moving through thick mud most of the time."
